(2 and 11, low) the CLI child env kept `RETEST_LINK_SECRET`, `CRON_SECRET` and `REPORT_SIGNING_KEY`: `STRIPPED_ENV` now spreads `lib/env.ts` `DERIVABLE_SECRETS` (plus `REPORT_SIGNING_KEY_CREATED`), tested. | 2026-10-20 | **Integration: p7/t8-mobile merged into main, the app lined up with the real `/api/v1`, and CORS for the Expo web build in local development only (spec change: domain §4.1 "Browsers").** The merge had one conflict, domain §11 (T8's N4 note on the local Expo prototype kept with T6's "beyond the clause 17 panel (§10)"); tsconfig, eslint and .vercelignore leave `mobile/` out, and `mobile/node_modules`, `.expo` and `dist` are ignored by mobile/.gitignore. **(a) mobile/src/api.ts only** (the screens are unchanged): the `Receipt` scheme (it was `Bearer`, which the server answers 401); the server's bodies are strict, so `serverReportBody` sends `followUp` (from the app's wantsRetest/emailChoice: email, else code, else none), `summaryChoice` and `previewToken`, and never `locationId`, and `serverRetestBody` sends `{ answer, comment }` without the app's own `source` (each would have been a 400); the parse functions read `{ organization, place }` (the poster's place becomes the app's location, with the web form's own prefill words and the `loc` the app asked about), `previewToken`, `reference`, the status's `organization` and `retest.offerText`, and errors as `{ error: { code, message } }` (401 `missing_code` reads as not found; 429 `org_full` as failed, since waiting a few minutes would not help). **(b) CORS:** new lib/api/v1/cors.ts, called by proxy.ts (its matcher gains `/api/v1/:path*`; staff pages unchanged): only for the origin exactly `http://localhost:8081`, only in `AUTH_MODE=local`, never on Vercel, and only for a request for this computer with no forwarding header pointing elsewhere (`localRequestAllowed`, the local sign-in's own test). A preflight is answered 204 by the proxy (`GET, POST, OPTIONS`; `Accept, Authorization, Content-Type`; max-age 600); the route's answer gets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` and exposes `Retry-After` and `WWW-Authenticate`; never `Allow-Credentials` (the API has no cookies). Any other origin, mode or host: no CORS header at all, as before (e2e's evil.example preflight still gets nothing). Tests: tests/api-v1-cors.test.ts (18) and a new e2e test in e2e/api-v1.spec.ts. **(c)** `GET /api/v1/orgs/{slug}` already exists on main (T4, with `?loc=` and the `public_report` bucket), so nothing was added. **(d)** In mobile/: `npm install` (the lockfile's own name fields now match package.json), `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo export --platform web` pass. | 2026-10-20 | **Abuse and spend limits (Phase 7 T3 item 7), all in lib/rate-limit.ts, without weakening them where they matter and without the e2e suite ever meeting them.** (1) **IPv6 by /64:** the per-client key hashes the address's bucket (`rateLimitBucket`: IPv4 as is, IPv4-mapped IPv6 as its IPv4, IPv6 as its first 64 bits, e.g. `2001:db8:1:2::/64`; anything else `unknown`, one shared and so stricter bucket). (2) **Platform-aware client IP** (`clientIp`): on Vercel `x-real-ip` (set by the platform), else its `x-forwarded-for`; anywhere else the LAST `x-forwarded-for` entry, the one the nearest proxy appended, never the first (whatever the client wrote) and never `x-real-ip` (client-settable without a proxy that overwrites it); entries normalized (brackets, port, zone, case) and checked with node:net `isIP`. Behind two proxies the last entry is the outer proxy's, so all clients share a bucket (strict, never open). `next start` keeps a client-sent `x-forwarded-for` as is, so locally a client can still pick its own bucket (README "Limits" says so; e2e relies on it for fresh 127.x buckets). (3) **Preview limit 20 per 10 min** (`RATE_LIMITS.intake_preview`), **except with the fake backend: 120**, the old value (`routeLimit`): the review's reason for lowering it is AI spend ("each 2-3 model calls"), and a fake preview pays for nothing; Playwright, CI and Vercel previews all run `FAKE_LLM=1`, so the suite (which sends many previews from 127.0.0.1) keeps its old headroom, locally and against a preview URL, while production (a real backend) gets 20; an unknown `AI_BACKEND` counts as real. (4) **Global AI budget** `AI_GLOBAL_HOURLY_LIMIT` (default 300, a whole number, `0` = no AI on the public form; checked in lib/env.ts): the preview action reserves its calls first (`reserveAiForPreview`: 2, or 3 with "Where exactly?") in one row `budget:ai_calls` per clock hour with a conditional atomic upsert (a refused request adds nothing, so exactly the limit gets through, also under concurrency); only real backends are counted. Past it, or if it can't be checked (fail closed for spend), the preview runs no AI and returns `{ ok: true, summary: null, token: null, aiPaused: true }`: no token, so "Send report" keeps layer 1 and sets needsManualRedaction (domain §5's manual path); the reporter's `aiOptOut` is not touched; the review says `AI_PAUSED_MESSAGE` ("Many people are using this form right now, so we did not make a short summary. A staff member will read your words. You can send your report as usual."). Staff-logged feedback and report phrasing (signed-in leads) are not counted: the budget guards the one AI path anyone on the internet can trigger; Anthropic's console spend limit (Wave 2, humans) caps everything. (5) **Per-org daily cap** `ORG_DAILY_SUBMISSION_LIMIT` (default 200) on web reports, one row `org_web_reports:{orgId}` per calendar day in Toronto (`zonedDayStart`; the DST days tested), counted in "Send report" after every other check (so a refused or invalid report never uses a place), exactly the limit a day; past it nothing is saved and the reporter reads `orgFullMessage` ("This form has taken all the reports it can for today. Your answers are still here. Please try again tomorrow, or tell {org} by phone at {phone} or by email at {email}."). Staff-logged feedback is never capped. **The one local allowance:** the cap neither counts nor refuses requests from this computer in the local prototype (`localLoopbackClient`: AUTH_MODE=local, VERCEL unset, a loopback address), impossible on Vercel (lib/env.ts refuses AUTH_MODE=local there) and through any tunnel or proxy (it appends the real, non-loopback address): the suite submits dozens of web reports per run, and the integration step runs it several times a day. (6) **Dashboard:** under the deadline banner one line `data-testid="intake-cap"` ("Web reports today: N of the 200 the form takes each day, to keep it from being flooded.", plus "In this local prototype, reports sent from this computer are not counted." locally), a warning Notice once reached (app/(staff)/app/dashboard/IntakeCap.tsx; read from rate_limits, no tenant data). **Files outside T3 touched (small, for the integration step):** app/o/[orgSlug]/report/actions.ts (budget, cap, email), ReportFlow.tsx (one ternary: the paused message), lib/intake/types.ts (`aiPaused`), the dashboard page (one line). T4's JSON API (T4.6) should call the same `limitRequest("intake_preview")`, `reserveAiForPreview` and `limitOrgSubmission`. Tests: tests/rate-limit.test.ts, tests/intake-limits.test.ts (the actions' wiring), e2e/platform.spec.ts (the dashboard line, axe), and db:smoke "app role takes from a shared rate_limits counter only within its limit" (the conditional upsert as accessloop_app under RLS; 36 checks now) | Review (High): "No AI spend cap: 120 previews per 10 min per IP, each 2–3 model calls; IPv6 gives unlimited buckets. A timed-out send is `failed` ("no answer from Resend within 5 s" in the log, never an address). The address and the rendered email (which may hold a receipt code) live in memory between the two steps, never in a table. Callers: the web report's ack (its own reporter transaction, then deliver), the re-test request after a logged fix (lib/cases/after-fix.ts: lead transaction FOR UPDATE, then deliver; its result type unchanged), and the case page's "Send acknowledgement email" and "Send a reminder email" (a small `emailStep()` in app/(staff)/app/cases/[ref]/actions.ts: queue inside onCase, deliver after, word the answer from the real result). **In flight:** a `pending` row younger than 2 minutes (`PENDING_IN_FLIGHT_MS`) for the same case and template makes the queue functions answer `already_sent` (the callers lock the case row, so a second click waits for the first commit and sees it), so the one-reminder-per-round and once-per-round rules hold during a send; an older pending row is a send whose process stopped, does not block, and shows in the Outbox as "Not confirmed yet". **The one exception:** staff-logged feedback's receipt-code email stays INSIDE the createCase transaction (`sendIntakeEmail`, now typed for `receipt_code` only, via `sendEmailInTransaction`), limited to the same 5 s: if it fails the whole intake must roll back, because nobody could ever see that code again and the immutable `submitted` event already says `codeRelayedByStaff: false` (the 2026-10-19 row "Which emails go when"); after a commit there would be no honest way to hand the code to anyone. No new bucket, so lib/rate-limit.ts (T3's) is untouched and T3's changes (lower preview limit, /64 buckets, platform-aware IP) apply to the API as they are. **Receipt code:** returned exactly once (the 201 of `POST /reports`, with `receiptCodeSpelledOut` from lib/intake/receipt-extras.ts); it goes back only as `Authorization: Receipt <code>` (any case, hyphens optional, as /check accepts), never in a URL or body: `?code=` and a `code` field are ignored (401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Receipt`). The scheme is `Receipt`, not `Bearer`: it says what the credential is, a reporter's capability and not a session, and it never collides with T3's `Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}`. Unknown, malformed and expired codes are one 404 with /check's words. **Bodies:** `application/json` only (415 otherwise; a cross-site HTML form can't send it without a preflight), at most 64 KiB read as a stream (413), strict zod (an `orgId` or any unknown key is 400, naming the field, never echoing a value). `followUp` (`none`/`code`/`email`, step 3's one question) instead of `wantsRetest` + `emailChoice`, mapped by the web form's own `followUpChoice()`; `email` is used only with `email` and otherwise never stored. **No CORS headers at all**: the native app needs none, and a page in a visitor's browser gets nothing. **Added beyond the brief's four routes:** `GET /api/v1/orgs/{slug}` (the public profile of /p/{slug} plus the form's answers and limits in the web form's words), because the app must name the organization and offer its other ways to reach it before anything is sent (Wave 3: choose an organization by slug or a QR link). **Not done:** no idempotency key, so an app that retries a `POST /reports` after a lost answer files a second report (Wave 3 must not retry it automatically); intake through the API is channel `web` (`intake_channel` has no `app` value and db/schema.ts is T2's); T3's global AI budget, if it lands in the preview server action, must be added to the preview route too (or, better, inside buildPreview). (b) a case closed BEFORE triage (no area, no public summary) reopens to `new`: a triaged case always has both, so "closed -> triaged" can't apply to it, and a mistaken spam close must be recoverable; (c) the replay (lib/metrics/replay.ts) clears `closedReason` when a case leaves closed and `verifiedAt` on a reopen from verified; the first `triaged` event stays the triage time and a retriage does not restart the assigned clock; Both flags work with any `--set` (injection and silver arrive from other branches) and with `--baseline`; a baseline pairs only with a run of the same layer-1 mode. Every new result also keeps `callSummary` (lib/eval/usage.ts): calls, p50/p95 latency and input/output/cache token totals per AI function, and p50/p95 per report, over all passes, numbers only. The printed JSON adds `repeats`, `layer1Only`, `rates` (each rate's mean, `ci95` and min/max across passes) and `callSummary`. René-Lévesque Ouest", "1234, CH. DE LA CÔTE-DES-NEIGES"); PO Box / RR / C.P. / case postale; OHIP and other health card numbers after their keyword without version letters ("my OHIP number is 1234 567 890", "carte santé", "RAMQ ABCD 1234 5678") and the Ontario 4-3-3 grouping on its own; account numbers (5+ digits) after "account" / "acct" / "compte" / "chequing" and 5-digit transit numbers after "transit" / "branch number"; any 9 digits after "SIN" / "NAS" and a joining word or sign, Luhn-valid or not; dates of birth after born / DOB / date of birth / né(e) / date de naissance in numeric, named-month (EN/FR) and month-year formats (type `DOB`); IT-service ticket IDs (INC0012345, RITM..., not inside a link's path, so a staff evidence link to a ticket stays allowed) and ticket / booking / case / candidate / client numbers after their keyword (type `OTHER_ID`, at least 5 letters and digits with a digit, never a `BAR-` case ref; "order 12345" stays visible as the existing test pins). The keyword stays visible, only the value becomes `[TYPE]`. **"SIN 123 456 789" with nothing between keyword and digits stays Luhn-checked**, because the existing grow-only test ("SIN needs a valid Luhn checksum") pins exactly that string as not redacted; "my SIN is", "SIN:", "SIN number", "NAS est le" and the long forms take any 9 digits. Every separator run is bounded, so no pattern can backtrack exponentially: the first draft's unbounded joins hung on 2,000 repeated "number is :" words (found by a benchmark, now a test). Measured on the visible set (fake run `eval/results/2026-10-20-fake-d654e056-3.json`; saved as `2026-10-20-fake-d654e056.json` on p7/t1-ai and renamed at the merge, because main already had a run of that name): 13 of 17 items removed before human review (was 11; F07's candidate ID and F08's booking reference now go at layer 1), 0 of 8 `notPii` items hidden. | 2026-10-20 | **The report sentence validator refuses swapped numbers, padded sentences and sweeping words (phase7-prompt T1 item 6), before real AI rewords anything.** lib/report/validate.ts compares a phrased sentence with its claim's template filled with the slot values: the numbers that are template values must appear in exactly the template's order (`number_order`; skipped when a slot is already `dropped_slot`, so one reason, not two); the sentence may have at most 1.3 times the template's words, rounded up (`too_long`, integer maths; "~1.3x" in the brief, implemented exactly); and a banned word may appear no more often than in the template (`banned_word`): compliant, compliance, certified/certify/certification, guarantee(s/d/ing), fully, every/everyone/everybody/everything/everywhere, all, 100% (also "100 %", "100 per cent", "cent pour cent"), satisfied, resolved, and the French conforme(s), conformité, certifié(e)(s), garanti(e)(s)/garantir/garantit/garantissons, entièrement, pleinement, totalement, chaque, tout/tous/toute(s), satisfait(e)(s), résolu(e)(s). A quoted public summary is stripped from both sides, so its own "every week" never licenses an added "every". Without a claim to compare against, any banned word is refused. The three sentences the review saw accepted are unit tests (tests/report-validate.test.ts): "... As a result, we are fully compliant with the Accessible Canada Act." (banned_word + too_long), "Every barrier reported this year has been removed." (banned_word), and two numbers swapped (number_order); each falls back to its template. lib/report/types.ts `REJECT_CODES` gains the three codes (a T5 file, an additive change). lib/ai/prompts/phrase-claims.md v2 tells the model the same three rules, so fewer real rewordings fall back (phraseClaims is not in the eval; its prompt sha changes in report events). tests/report-core.test.ts: the number-word check "That is four in all." now says "in total" (its point is the cited number word; "in all" is a banned word now). Every template fill still passes every rule (both report test files check that) | A real model could have published "fully compliant" or two swapped counts in a regulator-facing report; CLAUDE.md never allows "compliant", "certified" or "guarantees" in copy | Agent (Phase 7 T1) |
| 2026-10-20 | **api request shape (phase7-prompt T1 item 1, the review's BLOCKER): `tool_choice: auto` with a strict tool, no temperature, `effort: low` for structure, one jittered retry on 429/529, usage per call.** Checked on 2026-10-20 against Anthropic's documentation (platform.claude.com: the API errors page, the Claude Sonnet 5.5 and Claude Haiku 5.5 migration guides, structured outputs): a forced `tool_choice` (`tool`/`any`) returns HTTP 400 on Claude Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Fable 5.1 and Mythos 5.1 (Haiku 5.5 accepts it); Sonnet 5.5 rejects any non-default temperature and Haiku 5.5 accepts only `temperature: 1`; the docs' replacement for a forced tool is `auto` plus `strict: true` on the tool, whose JSON Schema may not use `minLength`, `maxLength`, `minimum`, `maximum`, `maxItems` (or `minItems` above 1); thinking is adaptive when `thinking` is omitted and `output_config.effort` is the lever (Sonnet 5.5 default `high`, Haiku 5.5 `medium`; `low` recommended for classification and extraction). **Now the default** (lib/ai/backends/api.ts): one tool, `tool_choice: {type: "auto"}`, `strict: true` with `strictToolSchema()` (those keywords removed and said in the description instead, `additionalProperties: false` on every object; the zod schema still validates every answer, so nothing is loosened), no temperature, no `thinking` field, `output_config.effort: "low"` for structure only (flagPii and phraseClaims keep the model's default); a plain-text answer without the tool call stays an AiOutputError. lib/ai/models.json gains `families` (Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5, Haiku 4.5: forced tool, sampling, effort, strict accepted or not); a model not listed gets the plain shape (auto, no strict, no temperature, no default effort). Knobs (.env.example): `AI_API_TOOL_CHOICE=auto\|tool`, `AI_API_TEMPERATURE=default\|0..1`, `AI_API_EFFORT_STRUCTURE` (default `low`) / `AI_API_EFFORT_PII` / `AI_API_EFFORT_REPORT`, `AI_API_EFFORT` for all three, `AI_API_STRICT_TOOLS=1\|0`; a knob the model is known to reject (e.g. `AI_API_TOOL_CHOICE=tool` on Sonnet 5.5, any temperature on the 5.5 models) is an AiConfigError before any request, and checkReady() checks all three functions, so the eval stops with that message instead of 400-ing into the fallbacks. **Retry:** HTTP 429 or 529 once more after 0.5-1.5 x 500 ms (or the server's `retry-after(-ms)` if longer), only when 2 s of the budget would still be left; the whole call, retry included, stays inside its 10 s (or the caller's sooner deadline); the SDK's own retries stay off; `attempts` is recorded. The 400 message now also names the Console spend limit (the API answers 400 when a spend limit set there is reached). **Usage:** input, output, cache-read, cache-creation and thinking tokens of every api and claude-cli call (the CLI's JSON result `usage`) travel in AiMeta and are saved per call in eval results with the latency (numbers only); intake stores none of it (the preview token and createCase keep the old meta fields). Supersedes the 2026-10-19 row "Known gap for a human: the api backend's domain request shape"; domain §6.1's api row says so. **Not verified:** no live call (no key; Wave 2's smoke test). CLAUDE.md's AI-contract line "api uses @anthropic-ai/sdk (tool use, temperature 0, 10 s timeout)" is now out of date; agents may not edit CLAUDE.md, so a human should change "temperature 0" to "no temperature, tool_choice auto" | The shipped defaults made every real call fail (HTTP 400) and fall back silently; the brief's safe default, refined by what the current documentation recommends | Agent (Phase 7 T1) |
| 2026-10-20 | **Intake AI budget (phase7-prompt T1 item 2, review N7): the two flagPii calls run in parallel and an intake's AI steps end within 10 s on api.** lib/intake/pipeline.ts `runIntakeAi()` (used by `buildPreview` and by staff-logged feedback, lib/intake/staff.ts, a minimal change there): layer 2 on the report and on "Where exactly?" start together; `structure()` starts as soon as the report's own layer 2 is done (it still only ever reads the layers 1 + 2 text); all three share ONE deadline = `intakeAiBudgetMs(backend)`: 10 s on api and fake (API_TIMEOUT_MS), 60 s on claude-cli (CLI_TIMEOUT_MS, domain §6.1 "stay accessible for up to 60 s"). The deadline travels as `AiCallOptions.deadline` (Date.now ms): lib/ai/core.ts refuses to start a real-backend call with under 1 s left (MIN_CALL_WINDOW_MS) and stops waiting at the deadline (AiTimeoutError), the api transport aborts its request there, and claude-cli never spawns a queued call whose turn comes with under 5 s left (CLI_MIN_START_MS) and shortens its 60 s kill timer to the deadline. A call that misses the deadline gets its usual fallback (layer 1 + needsManualRedaction; no summary), so "Send report" and the review behave exactly as after any AI failure. Before, the calls ran one after another: up to about 30 s on api (and 180 s on claude-cli) before the fallback. The eval runner gives each fixture the same budget, so its numbers describe what intake does. Tests: tests/intake-latency.test.ts (the real core and api transport with a stubbed slow client on vitest's fake clock: parallel starts; 8 s per call still ends by 10 s with layer 2 kept; a client that never answers ends at 10 s; structure not started with under 1 s left), tests/ai-core.test.ts, tests/ai-cli.test.ts, tests/ai-api.test.ts (deadlines) | Domain §6.1 limit 4 ("Never block the submit itself on the AI") and the review's latency finding; one budget is the simplest rule that bounds the wait whatever the model does | Agent (Phase 7 T1) |

(2) **`--json-schema` without `"$schema"`:** `z.toJSONSchema` writes the draft 2020-12 URI, and 2.1.299 rejects it before running ("--json-schema is not a valid JSON Schema: no schema with key or ref ..."). AccessLoop never runs a login and never switches backend. (5) **Recorded:** `cliVersion` (ai_structured payload, preview token, eval results) is the version with the capabilities as semver build metadata, e.g. `2.1.299+system-prompt-inline.no-max-turns.safe-mode`. (2) "One CLI call at a time" is now per server PROCESS: the queue lives on `globalThis` (`Symbol.for("accessloop.ai.claude-cli.queue")`), so two copies of lib/ai in a bundle, or `next dev` re-evaluating the module during a call, can't run two CLI processes. (3) The CLI child env also drops `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK` / `_VERTEX` / `_FOUNDRY`: like ANTHROPIC_API_KEY they would silently replace the subscription login (or send it and the report to another host). (4) A real model's very long answer (many layer-2 items, long `uncertain` notes) could seal a preview token longer than "Send report" accepts (16,000), which made sending fail with "Part of your report could not be read"; buildPreview now gives the shared fallback (no summary, no token, a text-free server line) instead, and `PREVIEW_TOKEN_MAX` is the one limit for the submit schema and openPreview. (5) `FAKE_LLM` is trimmed, so " 1" still forces the fake. The lists that grow on every run in the shared database (inbox, My work, Outbox) are cut at 3,000 px (the top of the list, at a readable size; My work was 39,688 px and 7 MB); any other page at 16,000 px. Cases come from e2e/fixtures (fileReport + moveCase; verdicts through /r/), one per variant for the just-verified screen, and every context sends its own made-up `x-forwarded-for`, so the spec never spends the public-form or /check buckets the rest of the suite (and a second run) uses from 127.0.0.1. Not serial: one variant failing never hides the others. e2e/screenshots/ stays out of .gitignore (eslint ignores it) | Phase 2 finish brief; CLAUDE.md "Visual QA"; two back-to-back full runs must stay under the rate limits | Agent (Phase 2) |

| 2026-10-19 | **Rate limits (lib/rate-limit.ts).** `rate_limits` rows keyed `hmacHex("IP_SALT", ip + "&#124;" + UTC date) + ":" + route`, fixed 10-minute windows aligned to the epoch (`window_start` = start of the window), one atomic `insert ... on conflict (key, window_start) do update set count = count + 1 returning count`; about 1 request in 50 also deletes windows older than 2 days. IP = first `x-forwarded-for` value, else `x-real-ip`, else "unknown"; never stored or logged. Over the limit: "Too many tries from this connection. Please wait a few minutes and try again." (report form: a warning notice on step 3 for the preview, the "not sent" notice for Send; /r/: a calm page; /check and /r/ answers: a focused notice). | 2026-10-19 | **Resolved 2026-10-19 by the approved scripts/local.ts change (top row).** **Known gap for a human: `next dev` and `next start` listen on every network interface** (they print `Network: http://<LAN IP>:3000`), and `localAuthAllowed()` trusts the `Host` header. So a device on the same network could send `Host: localhost` and reach the local "Sign in as…" page. The fix belongs in frozen `scripts/local.ts`: pass `-H 127.0.0.1` to `next dev` and `next start` (`nextBin(["dev", "-H", "127.0.0.1"], env)`, the same for `start`); `-H localhost` may bind ::1 only. Never expose the local build through a Host-rewriting tunnel | `scripts/local.ts` is frozen; the app code cannot see the socket's remote address, and the gap only exists on a shared network | Agent (Phase 1) |
